Adam and the other caliphs were called caliphs because the caliphate began with Adam, and those who rose after him carried out portions of the caliphate, and so were named accordingly — for whoever performs some portion of a thing is named after it. The Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him, fulfilled the reality of the caliphate in both the religious and the worldly sense. The Prophet, peace be upon him, said: "The sultan is the shadow of Allah on earth." Allah desired the Commander of the Faithful to be that shadow, for the shadow of a thing imitates the one casting it in everything. No one has dealt with creation as Allah Almighty deals with it except the Commander of the Faithful. The Prophet, peace be upon him, also said: "Allah lifts more through authority than through the Quran." And the Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him, is the one through whom Allah lifts what cannot be lifted by the Quran alone.

<!-- page 245 -->
[83] The philosophers said that philosophy is the imitation of the Almighty according to the capacity of human beings — that is, to act toward creation and toward oneself as the Almighty acts toward creation. This idea was appropriated by the philosophers from the people of truth; otherwise, neither philosophers nor anyone else could have affirmed the truth of this statement except the Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him. His entire dealing with creation mirrors Allah's dealing with His creation in the origination of the world, in sustenance, and in all else. We shall now mention something of that which befits the moment we are in.

[84] We begin by saying: the compassion of the Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him, his mercy, his benevolence, his pardon, and his tolerance of poor conduct among these people — despite their scant obedience — are such that no one like me can fully describe. All of this follows the actions of Allah Almighty

<!-- page 246 -->
in His creation and in obedience to the command of Allah Almighty, in conformity with His conduct. Allah, exalted above all, bestows His charity upon the righteous and the wicked, the believer and the disbeliever alike. For had He given the world to the believer and withheld it from the disbeliever, people would have embraced faith for the sake of the world and not for Allah and His religion. And had He withheld from all believers and given to the disbelievers, most people would have abandoned their faith and entered into disbelief. So He gives to the disbeliever and withholds from the disbeliever, gives to the believer and withholds from the believer, granting to each at one time and withholding at another. Likewise, the Commander of the Faithful gave to the Muslim and the unbeliever, and withheld from the Muslim and the unbeliever. He also gave to the believer and the hypocrite, and withheld from the believer and the hypocrite, granting to each at one time and withholding at another. For had he given everything to the believer, people's faith would have grown for the sake of the world; and had he withheld everything


<!-- page 247 -->
Believers at all times fall into despair and their intentions are discouraged. Allah has granted the believers a time of ease, unless no one grants it, and has withheld from them a good time. They are not among those concerning whom Allah Almighty has said: "No indeed, let man be overwhelmed by his sight, for his sight has been dispensed with." And Allah Almighty has given this world to the righteous and the wicked alike, so that holding fast to righteousness would not be for the sake of this world, nor clinging to immorality because of it, nor committing disobedience for the sake of this world, lest the fortune of the Hereafter be taken from them — but rather that all of this be purely for Allah and His religion. Likewise, the Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him, gives to the righteous and the wicked alike.

<!-- page 248 -->
Allah Almighty has given this world to those who obey Him and to those who disobey Him out of His wisdom, so that obedience would not be rendered for the sake of this world, nor would disobedience be avoided for its sake — but rather that all of this be sincere to Allah. In the same way, the Commander of the Believers, peace be upon him, gives to the disobedient and the obedient alike, following the example of the action of Allah Almighty, so that the obedience of creation to him would not stem from worldly considerations, lest the fortune of the Hereafter be taken from them.

[85] Allah has given this world to the worshipper and the non-worshipper alike, so that the creation would not worship for the sake of worldly gain. So too does the Commander of the Believers treat his creation — he does not look upon their corruption and disobedience, but rather regards them with the eye of mercy. Allah Almighty has given this world to the fool and the ignorant, and has given it also to the wise while withholding it from them, just as the Commander of the Believers gives to the foolish and the ignorant as well as to the wise and the virtuous.

<!-- page 249 -->
Had He given this world only to the wise and withheld it from others, people would have pursued knowledge for the sake of worldly gain, and the exercise of reason and its acquisition would have become means to worldly ends, and virtues would have been sought for worldly purposes, while the ignorant and the foolish would have fallen into despair. Allah gave this world to the fool and the ignorant, and gave it to the oppressor and the just, the generous and the miserly, the noble and the base, and to every possessor of virtue as well as to those lacking it — equalizing them all in the distribution of this world, out of His wisdom, mercy, and grace. The Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him, likewise made everyone equal in this, following the example of the deeds of Allah Almighty and out of mercy toward His creation, so that each one might reach his share of the benevolence and righteousness of the Commander of the Faithful.

[86] Allah Almighty has obscured the affairs of this world and the means by which creation attains from it, and the paths

<!-- page 250 -->
leading to it in all their forms, and has veiled from creation the means of gain and livelihood, so that one person goes without while another finds no bread, one prospers without trade while another loses his capital, one has no need of planting while another must sow, one is provided for without hunting while another is deprived — all of this for the wisdom He intended, so that no single path to this world would become so dominant as to render all other means void, introducing thereby a defect into creation and destroying the world. Likewise is the Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him, in his treatment of the creatures of Allah in this world — it becomes for them a reason

<!-- page 251 -->
whereby some are caused to receive by one means and others by another, and if they were to know of only one means, they would seek from the Commander of the Believers through that one means in this world, to the exclusion of all others, and those unable to avail themselves of it would despair of his mercy and be deprived. This would not have been a favor.

[87] The same applies to his treatment of people in drawing them near and keeping them at a distance. If Allah's peace be upon him were to drive away the hypocrite and draw the believer closer at all times, people would have become believers in order to be drawn near to him, not for the sake of religion. And if the believers were kept at a distance at all times, their hearts would be filled with fear of the wrath of the Commander of the Believers, peace be upon him, or dread of some error on their part. Rather, he treats them as time and circumstance require — with encouragement, warning, distancing, drawing near, giving, withholding, punishment, and pardon — all of it an imitation of the actions of Allah Almighty and His manner with His creation, so that people may remain

<!-- page 252 -->
always between fear and hope, neither so certain that they grow bold, nor so despairing that they lose all hope — in all of this following the manner of Allah Almighty with His creation. Just as Allah gives without reckoning and gives to those without fear of poverty or exhaustion of resources, so too do the Commander of the Believers, peace be upon him, give those who have no fear of poverty or the depletion of their wealth in giving to people.

[88] Whoever looks with the eye of truth at the actions of the Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him, in matters of religion and this world will find that everything he does with creation — commanding, forbidding, giving, and withholding — runs parallel to the actions of Allah Almighty. But this baffles people because they look at his deeds, peace be upon him, through the lens of the deeds of other human beings and measure the actions of the Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him, against the actions of all creation and the actions of kings and rulers. What they see, hear, or reason out appears to them different from what they are accustomed to and familiar with, and so they fall into confusion, bewilderment, and perplexity. If they were instead to look at that

<!-- page 253 -->
from the perspective of the actions of Allah with His creation, it would become easy for them. But it was difficult for them because no one has done this since Adam, peace be upon him, to this day. And when someone does it at some time, he does only a small part of it — as is narrated of what occurred with Moses, peace be upon him, who did not have patience for that because he saw it through the lens of his own self and his own knowledge and intellect, even as he was looking at it from the deeds of Allah Almighty. But it was contrary to what he had previously understood, as is narrated of the ruling of Dawud, peace be upon him, when Allah Almighty commanded him and he was struck with amazement and wonder at the hadith of the owner of generosity and the owner of the ox, as Allah Almighty informed him when He said: "On the Day when the caller calls to something dreadful" and "they were called to prostrate while they were" — and yet, despite this wonder, those people cannot bear these worldly acts. How then can they bear to be confronted with religious actions and with matters more difficult still? Everything that exists on both extremes is absent in the middle, and what exists in the middle is absent on both extremes, and people measure things by what they have seen

<!-- page 254 -->
in the middle while they themselves stand at the side — and for this reason they are confused and unable to bear it, nor do they have patience for it.

[89] We say: When people observed the gifts of kings, those gifts were either on account of service rendered, or kinship, or a duty owed, or love, or need, or the attraction of some benefit, or the warding off of some harm, or the response to a request, or because of obligation — that is, because they feared his evil or hoped for his good, or feared his tongue, or sought his praise and flattery, or wished to boast, or to elicit gratitude, or as wages for a worker and sustenance — and all of this is calculated and measured. The gifts of the Commander of the Believers, peace be upon him, are beyond all of that, because giving for any of these reasons

<!-- page 255 -->
cannot be called generosity, grace, or goodness. Rather, it is either the fulfillment of a past obligation or an advance for future benefit, and the withholding of what is owed and the discharge of rights is nothing but meanness and miserliness. Likewise, advancing money for some future purpose carries no generosity or grace, and what is given with a view to receiving gratitude in return is not counted as goodness, generosity, or favor. The giving of Allah is beyond all of that. No one has any prior claim upon Allah, and Allah asks nothing in return from creation. In the same way, the Commander of the Faithful gives to no one for any of these reasons, nor does he ask anything in return — as Allah Almighty has said, in a passage about the Commander of the Believers, Ali ibn Abi Talib,

<!-- page 256 -->
peace be upon him: "We desire no reward or thanks from you" — rather he gives out of originating grace and generosity, and as a virtue of following the actions of Allah Almighty with His creation. Had they looked at the actions of the Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him, in these ways, they would not have fallen into confusion. But some of them look at it with the eye of deficiency, measuring his actions against those of other creatures, while others look at him with exaggeration, calling him divine. He, peace be upon him, is the complete vicegerent of Allah over His servants.

[90] Just as Allah Almighty has concealed from creation the causes of this world while also revealing the causes of religion and the path to it — and the means of His satisfaction — explaining them through the words of His Prophet, peace be upon him, and granting knowledge and wisdom to His people while withholding them from those outside his family, and placing before them a barrier and behind them a barrier —


<!-- page 257 -->
Book of Proof of Imamate
p. 122.
And the guide to the bliss of the Hereafter, showing its causes and opening its doors to those who seek it.
It is given to those who deserve it and withheld from those who do not, so the Commander of
the Faithful concealed the causes of this world's affairs and directed them toward their rightful recipients, giving
to those who merited them and withholding from those who did not, while explaining the foundations of religion, knowledge, and wisdom and bestowing them upon
their proper custodians without prohibition or miserliness. He blessed creation and overflowed generosity upon them
as no one had done at any time before, and he forbade access to his household's prerogatives to all outside his family.
He distinguished the people of truth from the people of falsehood, following the example of God Almighty's own actions and manners in
the governance of religion, the world, and the building of the Hereafter and of what came before.
[91] We ask God to grant us gratitude for what He has bestowed upon us in word, deed, and intention,
and to help us obey the guardian of the age and time, may God's peace be upon him, and to grant us jihad
in his service with hand, tongue, and heart, and to open for him the east and the west of the earth.

<!-- page 258 -->
p. 123.
Book of Proof of Imamate
With his sword he cleanses the earth of disbelief, polytheism, and hypocrisy from end to end. He asks —
a servant who hopes the Commander of the Faithful, may God's peace and blessings be upon him, will bestow upon him the highest honor —
that by his attention the Imam gather for the servant his intimacy and permission to do what is right and proper,
and warn him of his error if he falls into one, so that the servant may give thanks and increase in insight.
This work is a guide for those who seek guidance and a proof against
the ungrateful. Praise be to God, the praise of the thankful, and prayers and blessings
upon our Messenger Muhammad and all his family.

the pure and the calm, and God is our sufficiency and the best deputy and the best protector and the best supporter, and there is no power but from God the Almighty,
the Great, b, w; +, and the void of his liberation, w.
The book was completed with God's help and praise, at the time of the afternoon prayer on Friday, the twenty-seventh day of the month of Rabi' al-Akhir in the year 1134 of the Hijrah, from Mecca, may God guard it.
God Almighty has completed the Book of Proof of the Imamate by the praise of God the King, with the help of his guardians who possess knowledge and exposition — Malik Khanbahi Tahfuji, residing in Bhanburi; and if his claim is false, a.
The book was completed in the lesson of our master Zain al-Din, may his station be exalted, in the year 1204, written by Malik Abdul Ali, student of Shaykh Baha'i, from Bhanburi, b.
This book was completed on the night of the sixteenth of the holy month of Sha'ban in the year 1259, in the hand of Abdul Maulana Najmuddin — may God exalt him — Ismail ibn Mulla Khan Bahari Diwan, at the request of Mullah Ghulam Hussain ibn Mian Khan Amil Andor, may God bless him and grant him peace, by the writing of my master and teacher Mia Sahib Abdul Muhsin — may God protect his high station — upon our master Muhammad and his pure progeny, may God's prayers be upon him.
